# Branch dispositions — the ONLY branch-disposition authority for this repository

Authority: `GOVERNANCE_REWRITE_2026_06_10/LETBUYY_CONSTITUTION.md` > root `AGENTS.md` > this ledger.
Operating rules: `.claude/skills/letbuyy/references/merge.md` (rows, claims, worktree lifecycle) and
`references/promote.md` (the `## Promotions` table). Enforced by
`scripts/validators/branch-dispositions-validator.mjs` (gate lane).

## Claim protocol

1. **The row is the claim, and it is the branch's first commit.** Before any implementation commit,
   add a row with `Status = ACTIVE`, the base SHA, and a `Why` that names the files you expect to
   touch and the collision check you ran (`git worktree list` · `git branch --list 'letbuyy/*'` ·
   the `ACTIVE` rows below, read for topic overlap). A row on an unmerged branch is invisible from
   `develop`, so the collision check is mandatory, not optional.
2. **Never change another branch's `Status` cell.** Flag and ask.
3. **Commit ledger edits immediately and with a pathspec** (`git commit -- docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md`).
   Several sessions share the primary checkout; a bare `git commit` sweeps other sessions' staged files.
4. On merge, the bookkeeping commit on `develop` sets `Status = MERGED_DEVELOP`, fills `SHA` with the
   branch tip, and `Merged into` with the merge commit. No placeholder may survive the merge.

## Status tokens

| Token | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `ACTIVE` | claimed, unmerged; the `SHA` cell is the base at claim time until the bookkeeping commit |
| `MERGED_DEVELOP` | contained in `develop`; `Merged into` = the merge commit |
| `ENVIRONMENT` | `develop`, `staging`, `main` themselves; moved only by merge (develop) or fast-forward promotion |
| `LEGACY_INTEGRATED` | fully contained in `develop` at adoption of the branch model; never advance, never delete |
| `PRESERVED_REFERENCE` | holds commits absent from `develop`; never merge without a new owner decision; never delete |
| `SUPERSEDED_BY:<branch>` | replaced by another branch; kept as history |
| `ARCHIVED_TAG:<tag>` | tagged and retired |

## Branch model (OWNER_DECISION 2026-09-04, D-BRANCH)

`letbuyy/<phase>` → `--no-ff` merge into `develop` (inside the develop worktree) → fast-forward-only
promotion `develop` → `staging` → `main`, each promotion with the owner's word in the same message.
`main` never receives a merge commit or a direct commit. `stepup/main` is a preserved alias of the
former integration branch and is never advanced again.

**Three branches, one checkout (OWNER_DECISION 2026-09-24, D-THREE-BRANCHES).** Between phases the repository
holds exactly `develop`, `staging` and `main`, in the primary checkout alone. A phase branch and its worktree
exist only while the phase is in flight. After the bookkeeping commit the branch is deleted with
`git branch -d`, which refuses an unmerged branch; its tip stays in the row's `SHA` cell and its commits stay
reachable from `develop`. So a `MERGED_DEVELOP` row normally names a branch that no longer exists, which the
validator already accepts. A branch holding commits absent from `develop` is never deleted: it becomes an
`archive/*` or `preserved/*` tag first, as the 2026-09-20 consolidation did.

The merchant path must come out behaviourally identical, and there is real evidence available for that: **23 existing tests** across `identity-issuer-d3.test.ts` (7) and `identity-redteam.test.ts` (18) cover it and all pass today, so the refactor is provable rather than hoped. **The security property this phase exists to establish** is cross-realm rejection: a merchant token must never verify as a buyer session and a buyer token must never verify as a merchant one. Separate audiences (`letbuyy:merchant-session` vs `letbuyy:buyer-session`) plus the realm/`token_use` pair are what enforce it, and both directions get an explicit test rather than being assumed from the constants differing. A buyer token also carries **no `site_id` or store claim** — a buyer identity is platform-level and is not a tenant credential; putting a site in it would quietly make it one. The fix keys on the identifier alone when no store is present (`identity:platform:<identifier>`), keeping the existing `identity:<storeId>:<identifier>` shape byte-for-byte when a store IS present — so it is **purely additive**: it adds a key where there was none and changes none that existed. **Scope note, recorded because it corrected this phase's own plan**: BUYER-3's register/login routes belong in `apps/identity-platform`, not the gateway — `identity-platform` is the sole token issuer (ART-05) and already owns `/api/auth/login` and `/api/auth/signup`, so minting a buyer session in the gateway would make it a second issuer, the exact violation a previous phase closed as an S0. Scope: define the function with the contract both call sites already describe, plus a **contract test pinning its call-site allowlist**, because `tenant-isolation-validator`'s known `const db = getSupabaseAdmin(c)` blind spot means nothing else would stop this becoming a general-purpose escape hatch. Merged into `develop` as `de769df5` (2026-09-05). `pnpm --filter @letbuyy/api-gateway build` goes exit=2 -> exit=0; gate on `55393b8c` failed=6 with zero new failing names and one fixed. |

Nothing canonical covers it: `app_featured_placements` (migration 100) is keyed by `app_id`/`developer_id` and belongs to the **frozen app marketplace** — the same false-positive shape that `app_versions` had in CAP-FB-APPUPDATE — and the `promotion_*` family is discount rules, not merchandising imagery. The client also **writes** banners directly to Firestore (`createBannerWithFile`, `updateBanner`, `deleteBanner`, `toggleBannerStatus`), so the canonical form is an operator-realm API, mirroring `operations/marketplace-categories.ts` — itself an operator-curated global list on `review:view` / `review:decide`. Scope: add `--continue` to the root `typecheck`, `lint` and `build` scripts and pin it with a contract test, so a single package can never again hide the rest. **Explicitly NOT in scope — an owner/ARB decision:** deleting the retired `cashfree`/`paypal`/`stripe` adapters. They are the sole cause of the remaining red, they have **zero production importers** (the only references outside the package are tests reading them as source to assert retirement), and `payments.test.ts:126` records the intent in the repository itself — *"deleting them is a separate SAFE_DELETE gated on zero importers"*. `CLAUDE.md` forbids deleting payment code without ARB, and widening `PaymentProviderName` to admit them is forbidden by `payment-rail-allowlist-validator` phase (c), so the red stays until the owner rules. | `letbuyy/soc1-seller-follow-graph` | `686fe4ab` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-05 | CAP-FB-SOCIAL (this phase) | Sixth of **D-9**'s areas, taken before ADS so the feed's *signals* exist before its paid *placements* and feed composition is not reworked twice. The button reports a state it never stores; the profile is lying to the buyer today. **(2) The only real implementation lives in the blocked area's service** (`reel_service.dart` `followSeller`/`unfollowSeller`/`isFollowing`/`getFollowerCount`) and keeps **two follower counts** — a subcollection `.count()` *and* a denormalised counter incremented on `sellers` — the two-sources-of-truth shape this programme keeps finding. Canonical design therefore makes the count a **projection**, never a second authority. Scope: migration 229 `seller_follows` (buyer_account → site, platform-owned like `buyer_store_customers` in 223), buyer-realm follow/unfollow/list routes behind `requireBuyerSession`, and a public follower count. Following an unpublished or suspended site must be **impossible in SQL**, not checked in a route a later path could bypass. Out of scope: the Flutter client (**D-10**), and exposing follower identities to merchants — a count is enough and the identity link is a deliberate privacy decision. (3) **The popularity signal is NOT greenfield and will NOT be built here.** `product_details_screen.dart` increments a `viewCount` field on the product document, but `analytics_events`, `analytics_event_stream` and `analytics_rollups_daily` already own product-view analytics canonically. Adding a `view_count` column to `products` would be a second source of truth competing with the analytics pipeline — the exact "two implementations, one wired to nothing" failure this programme keeps finding — so this phase records the owner instead of inventing a rival. Out of scope: the Flutter client (**D-10**). Merged into `develop` as `dc550181` (2026-09-06); gate on `cd667d65` failed=5, validators 111/113, typecheck green, zero new failing names. |

Measurement finds **no buyer↔seller chat anywhere**: no `seller_chats` collection, no `contactSeller`, no such screen — the only chat surfaces in `customer_app` are an **AI assistant** (`ai_chat_service.dart`, 5 screens) and **support** (`support_chat_service.dart`, 4 screens). Building buyer↔seller messaging would be **inventing a capability, not porting one**, and D-9 authorises porting. What is real is support chat — and it **already has a complete canonical owner**: `support_tickets` supports `requester_type = 'customer'` and `channel = 'chat'`, and `ticket_messages` carries `author_type` (requester/agent/system) and `visibility` (public/internal). Firestore's `support_conversations` is a duplicate of a system this repository already has. So this phase **wires** the buyer realm onto the canonical tickets rather than creating a parallel conversation model — the "two implementations" failure this programme keeps finding. **The security property that shapes it: a ticket message with `visibility = 'internal'` is an agent's private note and must NEVER reach the buyer**, so the buyer projection excludes it at the query, and a source contract fails if that predicate is removed. The **AI assistant is out of scope** (an LLM concern, not a Firestore port). Out of scope: the Flutter client (**D-10**). | `letbuyy/loy1-merchant-funded-loyalty-ledger` | `80faff9a` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | CAP-FB-LOYALTY (this phase) | Ninth of **D-9**'s areas, unblocked by **D-17** (owner, in chat 2026-09-06): loyalty is **merchant-funded and opt-in**, redemption is **TENDER** against the canonical ledger, and the balance is a **ledger projection** — never the mutable `points` field System A updates (`loyalty_service.dart` does `newBalance = points + earned` then writes it back, so a lost update silently mints or destroys money). Genuinely greenfield: no canonical loyalty table exists. Mirrors the gift-card family exactly — `gift_card_ledger_entries`' shape (`entry_type`, `amount >= 0`, `balance_after >= 0`, `idempotency_key NOT NULL`) and its `SUM(CASE entry_type ...)` projection — because a gift card is the repository's existing answer to "a stored balance spent as tender". **Scope is deliberately the ledger substrate and its RPCs, NOT the checkout integration.** `create_checkout_order` has been redefined by **eight** migrations, and 218 exists solely to **restore the idempotency advisory lock that 213 and 215 silently dropped** — a ninth redefinition is the highest-risk edit available here, and wiring tender into it deserves a dedicated phase that diffs all eight definitions and proves every prior fix survives. This phase therefore ships: `loyalty_programs` (merchant opt-in + rate), `loyalty_accounts` (per site + buyer, per **D-17**'s accepted cost that points are per-merchant), `loyalty_ledger_entries`, and idempotent `SECURITY DEFINER` award/redeem RPCs, plus buyer read routes. **The checkout wiring is named, not hidden** — until it lands, no order earns or spends points. Out of scope: the Flutter client (**D-10**). Merged into `develop` as `b9482e58` (2026-09-06); gate on `ec05a84d` failed=5, validators 111/113, typecheck green, zero new failing names. |
| `letbuyy/ads1-prepaid-ad-credits` | `d3101059` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | CAP-FB-ADS (this phase) | Tenth of **D-9**'s areas and the **last buildable one** — REELS stays blocked on the RESERVED `media-service` root. Money, governed by **D-5**: campaign credits are **prepaid only**, a *non-withdrawable, non-transferable service entitlement with their own ledger liability account, funded by a Razorpay purchase, never from sales payable*. Genuinely greenfield: `app_featured_placements` is the frozen app marketplace again and `marketing_email_campaigns` is email. System A's `AdCampaignModel` carries **`spentToday` and `spentTotal` as mutable counters** — the same read-modify-write shape CAP-FB-LOYALTY just replaced, where concurrent spend silently loses itself — so spend here is a **ledger projection**, reusing 231's pattern (append-only entries, one projection function, idempotent advisory-locked `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs on seed 3). Scope: `ad_credit_accounts` + `ad_credit_ledger_entries` (the D-5 liability account), `ad_campaigns` with its budget **allocated from credits**, `ad_campaign_placements`, and the public sponsored read the client actually performs (`marketplace_ad_provider.dart` reads active campaigns then their active products; nothing in this repository writes them). **Explicitly OUT of scope and named, not implied:** per-click/impression METERING (CPC/CPV/CPM), bidding and auction, and daily-budget reset jobs — System A's client only ever *reads*, so there is nothing to port there, and inventing an auction is not what D-9 authorises. The Razorpay **purchase wiring** is also a follow-on: the credit RPC takes a verified payment reference, and building a second payment path would violate the canonical-ledger rules this phase is written to respect. Merged into `develop` as `578f32a7` (2026-09-06); gate on `9f96885b` failed=5, validators 111/113, typecheck green, zero new failing names. |
| `letbuyy/chk1-checkout-definition-lineage-guard` | `91c44337` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-LOY-1 (part 1) — the control a ninth redefinition needs | **F-LOY-1** wants loyalty tender inside `create_checkout_order`. That function has **eight** definitions (073 → 219, growing 515 → 623 lines) and **218 exists solely to restore the idempotency advisory lock that 213 and 215 silently dropped** — a fix was lost twice and nothing caught it either time. **Audit result, measured first: 219 currently preserves everything 073→218 introduced** (a line-diff flagged three of 170's parameters as missing; they are present, the signature is simply formatted on one line in 219 — a false positive I checked rather than reported). So nothing is lost today, and the real gap is that **no test would notice if it were**. This phase builds that guard: a contract test that pins each definition's distinctive invariants — 206/218's `pg_advisory_xact_lock`, 213's place-of-supply and tax lines, 215's qualified digest under a restrictive `search_path`, 170's shipping-rate and tax parameters, 202's narrowed payment method, 219's parent/child purchases and its gift-card-as-tender rule — against **whatever the live definition is**, so the next redefinition that drops one fails immediately instead of shipping. Deliberately **does not** modify `create_checkout_order`: the guard has to exist and be proven before a ninth rewrite, not alongside it. The tender wiring is the follow-on it protects. Merged into `develop` as `13f48c83` (2026-09-06); gate on `dea6be11` failed=5, validators 111/113, governance green, zero new failing names. |
| `letbuyy/loy2-redeem-takes-authoritative-amount` | `823e9af1` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-LOY-1 (part 2) — make redemption safe to call from checkout | **Found by reading `create_checkout_order` before wiring it, not after.** 231's `redeem_loyalty_points` caps a redemption at `redeem_cap_bps` of **`orders.total`**. That is correct for a standalone call and **wrong for the checkout path**: inside `create_checkout_order` the order is created by `create_order_with_inventory_reservations`, and at that moment `orders.total` still holds the **pre-adjustment** figure — shipping, tax and discount are only folded into the local `v_grand_total` later, and `total` is not rewritten until the final `UPDATE orders ... total = v_payment_due`. Redeeming there would cap against a row that is **mid-mutation**, and the direction of the error depends on where the call is placed. A naive wiring would have produced a subtle money bug that no typecheck and no route test could see. It also compounds: line 292 sets `total = v_payment_due`, the amount left after gift-card tender, so "50% of the order" is ambiguous between the order's value and its residual. **Fix: the caller passes the authoritative payable amount.** `redeem_loyalty_points` gains `p_max_value_minor`, and the redemption is capped by the programme cap **and** that amount — mirroring the gift card's own `LEAST(balance, v_grand_total)`. The old signature is DROPped rather than overloaded: two versions of a money RPC is a second authority. Points are clamped **before** the debit, so a buyer is never charged more points than the value applied. **Does not modify `create_checkout_order`** — that ninth redefinition is the next step, and it is now unambiguous. Merged into `develop` (2026-09-06); gate on `3065c555` failed=5, validators 111/113, governance green, zero new failing names. |
| `letbuyy/loy3-checkout-loyalty-tender` | `823e9af1` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-LOY-1 (part 3) — the ninth definition | Loyalty applied as checkout TENDER. 219 TRANSFORMED rather than retyped; DROP+CREATE because added parameters make an overload, not a replacement. Protected by part 1 lineage guard and part 2 payable amount. Merged into `develop` as `8ce3d5da` (2026-09-06); gate on `b6cd320b` failed=5, validators 111/113, typecheck and governance green, zero new failing names. |
| `letbuyy/loy4-loyalty-earn-sweeper` | `0959518e` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-LOY-2 — points are spent but never earned | 234 made points SPENDABLE at checkout; `award_loyalty_points` still has **no caller**, so the cycle is half-built. **Where the hook goes was decided by measurement, not preference.** The obvious place is `confirm_order_payment_with_ledger`, but that function runs in ONE transaction: if awarding raised there, the whole capture would roll back **after the provider already took the money**, and a persistently-broken loyalty programme would make payments unretryable. The Constitution says the synchronous path performs only work required for a correct result — a loyalty accrual is not. So the earn side is **asynchronous**, and it needs no new event: `confirm_order_payment` **already emits `payment.confirmed`** atomically with the order update. Implemented as a queue-worker sweeper rather than an event consumer with a cursor, because **the ledger entry is its own marker**: the sweeper awards for paid orders that have no `earned` entry yet, so it is idempotent, self-healing, and cannot double-award even if it runs twice on the same order. Also fixes a latent hazard in 231: `award_loyalty_points` **raises** on a frozen or closed account, which for a synchronous caller would be a payment failure; it now returns `awarded: false` with a reason, consistent with how it already treats an inactive programme. Merged into `develop` (2026-09-06); gate on `0cf84a05` failed=5, validators 111/113, zero new failing names. |
| `letbuyy/guard1-buyer-owned-tables` | `8be934c2` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-GUARD-1 — buyer-owned tables sit outside the tenant guard | The registry generator registers a table only when it carries `site_id`/`store_id`, so buyer-owned tables (`buyer_notifications`, `buyer_product_views`) are covered by RLS and route scoping but **not** by the tenant query guard — a gateway bug that forgot the buyer predicate would dump every buyer's inbox or browsing history. **Measured before acting, and the measurement narrowed the change.** Relaxing the filter to any owner column would add **32** tables, including `sessions`, `verification_tokens`, `devices` and `merchant_session_jti_consumptions` — all legitimately looked up by a **globally-unique token with no owner predicate**, which is the documented shape `getSupabaseAdminUnguarded` exists for. Guarding those would break real lookups, so the blanket widening is **rejected on evidence**, not deferred. This registers only tables carrying `buyer_account_id` — the realm the finding is about, whose every existing query is already buyer-scoped — and proves it by running each real query shape through the real guard rather than reading them. Merged into `develop` (2026-09-06); gate on `5644dcc6` failed=5, validators 111/113, zero new failing names. Registry 276 → 278. |
| `letbuyy/disp1-row-placement-guard` | `eef24106` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-DISP-1 — the validator checks row content, not row placement | `branch-dispositions-validator` verifies every disposition row's SHA, status token and shape, and never checks **where the row is**. That is not hypothetical: on 2026-09-05 an insertion helper anchored on the string `## Promotions`, which line 5 mentions in prose (*"`references/promote.md` (the `## Promotions` table)"*), and spliced two claim rows into the middle of that sentence — splitting it across the rows. The validator passed on the corrupted file, and it was found by eye during a merge conflict. **Reproduced here before fixing**: re-splicing a row into the same sentence still exits 0. Adds a placement check — every `| \`letbuyy/…\`` row must sit inside `## Rows`, and the `## Rows` and `## Promotions` headings must each appear exactly once as headings — so the next unanchored insertion fails loudly instead of corrupting the ledger silently. Merged into `develop` (2026-09-06); gate on `8c5e82f2` failed=5, validators 111/113, zero new failing names. |
| `letbuyy/ledger1-stored-value-write-fence` | `0397dba8` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-LEDGER-1 (this phase) | **The two stored-value ledgers this programme just built are writable from TypeScript, and the validator that exists to prevent exactly that does not cover them.** `canonical-ledger-only-validator`'s `moneyMutationTables` names thirteen tables — `gift_card_ledger_entries` among them — and **none** of 231's `loyalty_accounts` / `loyalty_ledger_entries` or 232's `ad_credit_accounts` / `ad_credit_ledger_entries`. Worse than the missing lint: 231 and 232 both `GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ... TO service_role` on those tables, and 232 does the same for `ad_campaigns`, whose `allocated_minor` column is the campaign's spendable budget. So `getSupabaseAdmin(c).from('ad_campaigns').update({ allocated_minor })` would **mint ad credit with no ledger entry** today — the read-modify-write shape both migrations were written to abolish — and the database would allow it. Measured: **zero** TypeScript call sites mutate any of the five tables right now (all six existing `.from()` sites are `.select()`), so this closes the hole while it is still theoretical rather than after a route walks into it. Scope: migration 236 revokes `INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on the four stored-value tables from `service_role` (every writer is already a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC running as the owner, so nothing loses a capability it uses) and narrows `ad_campaigns` to a **column-level** `GRANT UPDATE (name, status, starts_at, ends_at, updated_at)` so `allocated_minor` moves only through `allocate_ad_campaign_budget`; plus a strict no-escape-hatch arm in `canonical-ledger-only-validator` and a forward ratchet so a later migration cannot silently re-grant. **`loyalty_programs` is deliberately NOT fenced here** — it is settings, its write path does not exist yet, and F-LOY-3 must add it as an RPC; that is named, not implied. Out of scope: any route, any client, and the four open product findings. Merged into `develop` as `d909d762` (2026-09-06); gate on `0397dba8` failed=4 against a re-measured `develop` baseline of failed=4 — **the ambient baseline is 4 now, not the 5 the previous rows record** — validators 111/113, typecheck and governance green, zero new failing names in either direction. | `d909d762` |
| `letbuyy/loy5-merchant-loyalty-program-settings` | `8f21d8ce` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-LOY-3 backend half (this phase) | **Loyalty is complete end to end and cannot be turned on.** 231 through 235 built the programme table, the ledger, both money RPCs, checkout tender and the earn sweeper — and `loyalty_programs` has **no writer anywhere**: measured, the only TypeScript references are two `.select()`s (`buyer-realm-wiring.ts`, `loyalty-earn.ts`), so a merchant can enable loyalty or set a rate only by hand-written SQL, and the earn sweeper's `is_active = true` filter means **no store will ever match**. This phase gives the table its canonical writer. **It must be an RPC, not a table update, for a measured reason:** `redeem_loyalty_points` reads `point_value_minor` **at redemption time**, so halving it halves the rupee value of every point already earned at that store — a liability revaluation that has to be attributable, not a silent `UPDATE`. Migration 237 therefore adds `set_loyalty_program`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` upsert that validates, records an `audit_logs` row carrying old and new values **plus the outstanding point balance and the resulting liability delta in paise**, and returns the row — the change and its audit trail atomic in one transaction, because a TypeScript-side audit write can fail after the change has landed. It also adds `loyalty_outstanding_points`, built **on top of** `loyalty_balance` rather than re-summing the ledger, so there stays exactly one definition of a loyalty balance. **Deliberately NOT decided here:** whether a merchant may devalue outstanding points at all — D-17 makes the programme merchant-funded, which argues yes, but the cost falls on buyers who already earned; recorded as an open owner question rather than answered by the code that happens to be easiest. **Also not re-invented:** `log_audit_event()`, the repository's existing audit trigger, is NOT attached — it records `auth.uid()`, which is NULL for the canonical merchant realm, and `COALESCE(NEW.id, OLD.id)` would fail outright on a table whose primary key is `site_id`. Gateway gets merchant-realm read and write routes; the read goes through the tenant transaction so RLS and the `app.site_id` GUC are exercised rather than bypassed. Out of scope: the merchant_web settings screen (**D-10**), and any change to the fence migration 236 put on the ledgers. Merged into `develop` as `47d24bf5` (2026-09-06); gate on `8f21d8ce` failed=4 against a `develop` baseline re-measured at `1f77be4e` in the same session (also 4), validators 111/113, typecheck green, zero new failing names in either direction. | `47d24bf5` |

That is a decision about how the platform charges a merchant, and it is the owner's. This phase makes it a gate failure. **The sweep is harder than it looks, and getting it wrong is the point of the design:** three hand-written passes over the same question each returned a WRONG answer in a different direction. Classified honestly: **not a credential exposure** — a Razorpay key *id* is a public client identifier, `rzp_live` count is **0**, so no rotation obligation — but a **latent production-mode hazard**, because an app that picked it up would run checkout in Razorpay **test mode**, where payments appear to succeed and settle nothing. Dead today (no app references `CustomPaymentService`); donor lineage awaiting **D-10**. **Two further corrections to the same section.** §7's first bullet blames `firebase_bridge/src/commissionService.ts` for `foundation-antifabrication-gate` — that tree was deleted and the gate now **passes**, so the bullet is stale. And `stale-artifact`'s offenders are re-confirmed **100% generated directories**. Durable control: a test that pins each known-red validator's offender list **by classification**, so a new offender joining a familiar red fails loudly instead of being inherited — and, symmetrically, a validator that starts **passing** also fails, so the register cannot go stale in the other direction either (which is exactly how §7's first bullet survived). Paths are extracted by matching what a repository path looks like rather than by guessing where the message ends. | `letbuyy/dart1-dead-source-reference-soundness` | `82bcf3b9` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-DART-1 (this phase) | **`dart-dead-source-validator` archives 37 files and then checks for references to them by BARE BASENAME — `source.includes('status_badge.dart')` — which is unsound in three distinct ways at once, all three present in the tree today.** (1) `apps/customer_app/.../product_card.dart` matches because its **own header comment names itself**; (2) `order_card.dart` matches `status_badge.dart` because it imports `order_**status_badge**.dart` — the archived name is a *suffix* of a longer, unrelated file; (3) `all_products_grid.dart` matches `product_card.dart` because it imports `compact_**product_card**.dart`. Widening it naively would flag **8** legitimate `customer_app` files — which is exactly why the soundness fix has to come first. | `letbuyy/ads1a-ad-spend-metering` | `54d82065` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-ADS-1a (this phase) | **Owner chose F-ADS-1 (2026-09-06, in chat), overriding the recommendation to leave it.** I had classified metering/bidding as inventing product scope rather than porting, since System A's client only ever *read* campaigns; the owner decided otherwise and this proceeds. **Measuring it reframes most of it as wiring, not invention:** migration 232 already defines `entry_type` `'spent'` and `'released'`, and `ad_campaign_spent_minor()` already projects spend per campaign — **the spend leg simply has no writer**, the same shape as the last four phases. **The headline finding is a double-debit that only exists once metering lands.** `allocated` debits the ACCOUNT balance, and `ad_credit_balance_minor` debits `'spent'` **again** — but that money already left the account when it was allocated to the campaign. Purchase 100000 → allocate 40000 (balance 60000, campaign 40000) → a 5000 spend would read balance 55000, while the merchant still holds 60000 unallocated and 35000 unspent: the arithmetic only closes if spend leaves the account balance alone. Invisible today **only because nothing writes a `spent` row**, so it is fixed before the first one exists. **A second piece of named scope dissolves on measurement:** System A's daily-budget RESET job existed only because `spentToday` was a mutable counter that had to be zeroed. With a ledger, "today's spend" is a query over a date window — there is nothing to reset and no job to build. Today is computed in **`Asia/Kolkata`**, not UTC, or an India-first merchant's day would roll over at 05:30. Scope: fix the projection; add `ad_campaign_remaining_minor` and `ad_campaign_spent_today_minor`; give `ad_campaigns` a `pricing_model` (`cpc`/`cpm`), a `bid_minor` and an optional `daily_budget_minor`; and add `record_ad_spend`, which computes cost **server-side from the campaign's own bid** (never from the caller), is idempotent on the shared seed 3, refuses a campaign that is not active or not in window, **clamps the last charge to the remaining allocation** rather than refusing it or overdrawing, and flips the campaign to `exhausted` — the status the merchant routes deliberately cannot set, because it is a fact about the ledger. Out of scope, and named: the click/impression **ingress** and feed ranking by bid (**F-ADS-1b** — a public endpoint that spends a merchant's money needs its own abuse controls), and second-price auction mechanics (first-price is what a merchant expects when they set a max bid; second-price is a pricing decision, not an implementation detail). Merged into `develop` as `2b0d725d` (2026-09-06); gate on `54d82065` failed=4 against a baseline measured at the exact base `066193a8` in the same session, zero new failing names. **The first gate run failed with 5**, both correctly: `tenant-isolation` (239 ALTERs `ad_campaigns`, so the registry needed regenerating — diffed first: 2 lines, count unchanged at 278) and **F-GOV-RPC-1's own reachability guard**, naming `record_ad_spend` as an RPC nothing calls. | `2b0d725d` |
| `letbuyy/ads1b-sponsored-feed-ranking` | `24698116` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-ADS-1b (this phase) | **The sponsored feed serves campaigns that cannot pay.** `marketplace_sponsored_products` filters on `status = 'active'` and the campaign window and nothing else — so a campaign whose allocation is spent, or which has hit today's cap, keeps being served, and F-ADS-1a's `record_ad_spend` would then decline every event it generates. Serving an ad nobody can be billed for is the read-path half of the same bug. **This is also the auction**, in the only form the schema supports: order by the merchant's own `bid_minor`, highest first. First-price, as recorded in F-ADS-1a — a merchant pays what they bid. Scope: migration 240 redefines the function to rank by bid and exclude any campaign that is exhausted, out of window, over its daily cap, or holding no remaining budget, reusing `ad_campaign_remaining_minor` and `ad_campaign_spent_today_minor` rather than re-deriving either. **The redefinition is the risk, so the guard comes first:** this function carries security-relevant visibility fences — a sponsored placement must not surface a hidden product, a soft-deleted one, or an unpublished seller — and every one is pinned in the lineage test, green against 232, **before** 240 is written. **Deliberately NOT in this phase: the click/impression INGRESS.** A public endpoint that spends a merchant's prepaid balance must be forgery-resistant, which needs a server-issued signed placement token and therefore a **dedicated signing secret** — a new trust domain, since SEC-R7 forbids reusing one across domains. That is buildable without touching deploy config if it fails closed when the secret is absent (the pattern `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` already uses), but it is its own piece of work and it has to bind a placement the feed actually served — which is what this phase makes true. F-ADS-1c. `record_ad_spend`'s reachability exemption therefore stays, and is re-pointed at F-ADS-1c. **The suite corrected the claim above**: 232's feed does NOT serve every spent-out campaign, because `record_ad_spend` flips one to `exhausted` and 232 filters on status. The daily cap is the genuinely new predicate; the remaining-budget one covers a merchant **re-activating** a spent campaign through the PATCH route. Merged into `develop` as `a381eb05` (2026-09-06); gate on `24698116` failed=4 against a baseline measured at the exact base `f603d81b` in the same session, zero new failing names. | `a381eb05` |
| `letbuyy/ads1c-signed-ad-event-ingress` | `3c057e85` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-ADS-1c (this phase) | **The last piece of F-ADS-1: an endpoint the public can call that spends a merchant's prepaid balance.** `record_ad_spend` has been callable-but-uncalled since F-ADS-1a, held by a reachability exemption this phase retires. The whole difficulty is forgery: anyone who can POST "click" spends someone else's money, so the endpoint accepts only a **server-issued, HMAC-signed placement token** binding site, campaign, product, an expiry and a nonce — the `timingSafeEqualHex` pattern the checkout handoff and the Razorpay verify already use. The nonce is the idempotency key, so `record_ad_spend`'s replay path makes one token bill exactly once. **The design constraint that decides the whole shape:** the sponsored feed is RESPONSE-CACHED (`cachedJson`, TTL + SWR). A token placed inside the cached body would be handed to every buyer who hit that TTL — one nonce, so one billable event per cache period rather than per impression, and every later buyer's click silently free. Tokens are therefore signed **outside** the cached region: the rows stay cached and shared, the tokens are minted fresh per response. Getting this backwards would have built a biller that undercounts by exactly the cache hit rate, and it would have looked like it worked. **A new trust domain, so a dedicated secret:** `AD_PLACEMENT_SIGNING_SECRET`, never a reused one (SEC-R7). It is declared **optional** and the path **fails closed** without it — no tokens are issued and the event endpoint answers 503, exactly as the Razorpay routes do — so this phase touches **no deploy configuration** and cannot reproduce the "production deploy hard-fails without a new secret" trap the media-upload secret caused. Wiring the secret is an owner act, recorded as one. Scope: the token helper, token issuance on the sponsored feed, `POST /v1/storefront/ads/event`, rate limiting, and retiring `record_ad_spend`'s exemption. Out of scope: attributing a click to a buyer identity (the feed is public and unauthenticated by design), and any client change (**D-10**). Merged into `develop` as `68b150c6` (2026-09-06); gate on `3c057e85` failed=4 against a baseline measured at the exact base `c121f945` in the same session, zero new failing names. **`record_ad_spend`'s reachability exemption is RETIRED** — it has a caller, and the guard now treats it as an ordinary governed function. **F-ADS-1 is complete.** | `68b150c6` |
| `letbuyy/bill1-subscribe-fabricates-payment` | `303eb166` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-BILL-1 (this phase) | **Found while measuring D-PLATFORM-CHARGE's third option, and it is a money-integrity defect in its own right.** `POST /v1/billing/subscribe` decides whether a merchant's subscription is paid from **`body.sandbox_payment_status`** — a client-supplied field that **defaults to `'paid'`** (`packages/billing-core/src/index.ts:126`) — and then activates the subscription with a **fabricated** provider reference, `sandbox_autopay_payment:<idempotency_key>`. There is no provider call, no signature check and no amount verification anywhere on the path. **It is not gated by mode:** `assertAutopayReady` is called at line 167 for `/mandate` **and nowhere else**, so `/subscribe` behaves identically whether `LETBUYY_AUTOPAY_MODE` is `sandbox` or `live`. An authenticated merchant can therefore activate any purchasable plan, on a live deployment, without paying — the Constitution's "never trust client payment status" and "a client-supplied payment identifier is never sufficient proof of payment" in one route. **Containment is owner-independent, which is why this is a phase and not a question:** in live mode `/subscribe` must refuse rather than fabricate. Sandbox behaviour is untouched, so nothing in development changes. What it must eventually DO in live mode is exactly **D-PLATFORM-CHARGE**, and this phase does not answer that — it stops the fabrication and registers the finding. **It also corrects the decision record:** `references/decisions.md` describes option 3 as riding `billing`'s existing mandate/autopay model. There is no model to ride — billing has no verified payment path either, so all three options are variations of "build one", which materially changes the choice. Out of scope: building that path, and the `/mandate` route, which does carry the gate. | `letbuyy/charge1-shared-payment-verifier` | `196ed4c3` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-CHARGE-1 (this phase) | **Executes D-PLATFORM-CHARGE (owner, 2026-09-06): ONE verification authority, parameterised by what holds the reference.** The only verified Razorpay path in the repository was welded to `orders` — it fetched the order, checked `payment_status`, and failed closed unless `orders.payment_id` matched the presented provider order id — so a merchant buying ad credits, which is not a storefront order, could not reuse it. The owner rejected an `orders` row per platform charge (the platform becomes a party in its own tenant's commerce, inside GST invoice scope) and rejected building UPI AutoPay first (blocked on `HR-AUTOPAY-LIVE`), choosing to **extract the same code and let the caller say where the reference lives**. That is not the second payment authority migration 232 forbids: a second authority means a second place that DECIDES whether a payment is real, and this keeps exactly one. **This phase is the extraction only — no new endpoint, no behaviour change.** Signature and the fail-closed binding move into `lib/provider-payment-verification.ts`; replay protection deliberately does NOT move, because `assertProviderPaymentNotReplayed` is bound to `payment_transactions` and answers a different question. **The interesting part is the guard.** `payments.test.ts`'s "validates signatures before confirming payment" was already GREEN and pinned this path — verified before relying on it, since one of that file's tests is an ambient red (the webhook one, a different subject). It anchored on the binding check's literal text **inside `payments.ts`**, so the extraction broke it — the guard noticing its subject moved. It is re-anchored **stronger, not looser**: it now pins the ordering across both files, pins the binding in its new home, pins that the route passes the SERVER-stored reference rather than anything from the body, and asserts the route kept **no second copy** of the HMAC construction. Out of scope: the platform-charge route and wiring `record_ad_credit_purchase` — F-CHARGE-2, which is what retires its exemption. **Protocol deviation, recorded rather than glossed:** the claim row and the implementation landed in ONE commit, so the claim was not this branch's first commit as `merge.md` requires. The collision check was run before work started and the row is correct, so the deviation is procedural — but the point of claim-first is that the row exists before the work, and here it did not. | `letbuyy/charge2-ad-credit-purchase` | `f2e06d9b` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-CHARGE-2 (this phase) | **Completes D-PLATFORM-CHARGE: a merchant can finally buy ad credits, and the last reachability exemption retires.** F-CHARGE-1 extracted the verifier; this binds it to `billing_history` and wires `record_ad_credit_purchase`, which has had no caller since F-ADS-1a. Two routes: an **intent** that creates the provider order through the existing `createRazorpayOrder` helper (already table-agnostic, so no second provider path) and records a **pending** `billing_history` row whose `payment_id` IS the server-created reference; and a **verify** that runs the shared verifier against that stored reference and, only then, calls the credit RPC. **They live in `routes/billing.ts`, not `routes/ads.ts`, for a specific reason:** `ads.ts` has a tested property that it contains **no service-role path at all** — everything runs inside the tenant transaction — and these routes need the admin client for the provider call and the billing row. Putting them there would have quietly broken that contract, and "bound to billing" is what the decision says. **The money properties:** the amount charged comes from the **stored billing row**, never the request; the site comes from the resolved owned-store scope AND the row must belong to it, so a merchant cannot verify a payment into someone else's balance; the row must still be `pending`; and the credit RPC's idempotency key is the **Razorpay payment id**, so one real payment credits exactly once — `ad_credit_ledger_entries`' `UNIQUE (site_id, idempotency_key)` is the backstop. Replay protection is deliberately NOT `assertProviderPaymentNotReplayed`: that is bound to `payment_transactions` and to an order id, and a platform charge has neither. Out of scope: subscriptions moving onto this path (the same verifier now serves them, but changing `/billing/subscribe`'s behaviour is a separate decision beyond containment), and the merchant_web screen (**D-10**). Out of scope, and classified rather than left silent: the other six unwritten entry types. **`released`** (ad credits) is a **real** sibling gap — ending a campaign strands its unspent allocation, since `allocated` debited the account and nothing returns it — and is registered as **F-ADS-4**. `expired`, `revoked`, `granted` and the two `adjustment_*` types are **reserved vocabulary awaiting owner decisions** (expiry policy, operator grants and manual adjustments), not defects. | `letbuyy/ads4-release-campaign-budget` | `a00a641f` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-ADS-4 (this phase) | **A merchant's prepaid credit can enter a campaign and never come out.** The sibling gap F-LOY-4's sweep found: `'released'` is declared in 232's `ad_credit_ledger_entries` vocabulary and **nothing writes it**, while `allocate_ad_campaign_budget` debits the account and raises `allocated_minor`. So a merchant who allocates 40000 to a campaign and ends it having spent 5000 has **35000 they cannot get back** — not lost to the platform, but locked in a campaign that no longer serves, with no path to any other campaign. Scope: migration 242 adds `release_ad_campaign_budget`, the exact inverse of allocation — `NULL` amount means release everything unspent (the case that matters when ending a campaign), it refuses to release more than `ad_campaign_remaining_minor`, it credits the account with a `'released'` entry **and** decrements `allocated_minor` in the same transaction so the two never disagree, on loyalty-free seed 3 with the replay path and tenant fence its siblings carry. Plus a merchant route. **Deliberately NOT done, and named rather than assumed: auto-release when a campaign is set to `'ended'`.** That is the behaviour a merchant probably expects, but it changes what a status change MEANS — a merchant who ends a campaign intending to reactivate it next month would find the budget silently returned — and 236's column fence exists precisely so `allocated_minor` moves only through a named money RPC. With an explicit release the credit is **recoverable**, which is the gap; making it **automatic** is a product decision. Out of scope: expiry of released credits (`'expired'` remains reserved vocabulary pending an owner policy). Merged into `develop` as `641aaa61` (2026-09-06); gate on `a00a641f` failed=4 against a baseline measured at the exact base `52707c61` in the same session, zero new failing names. | `641aaa61` |
| `letbuyy/loy5-record-value-at-earn` | `d34ed812` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-LOY-5 (this phase) | **Measured while preparing the D-LOY-REVALUE decision, and worth doing whichever way it goes.** `point_value_minor` is read at **redemption** time (233's `redeem_loyalty_points` for both the cap and the value, and 234's `create_checkout_order`), so lowering it revalues every outstanding point. The **redeem** entry records the value it used, in metadata. The **earn** entry records only `points` — **nothing about what a point was worth when it was earned**. So the system cannot today honour "the value you earned at" even if the owner chose that, because it was never written down, and it cannot reconstruct the history of a revaluation after the fact. **This phase does not answer D-LOY-REVALUE and deliberately changes no behaviour:** every redemption still values points at the CURRENT rate, exactly as before. It records `point_value_minor` and the derived `value_minor` on the `earned` and `refund_credit` entries so the fact is captured going forward. That is strictly good under all four options — free revaluation (it makes the swing auditable per entry, not just per settings change), value fixed at earn time, upward-only, or a notice window — and it is a **precondition** for the two that grandfather, which cannot be built at all until earn entries carry a value. **Forward-only, and stated as such:** entries written before this migration have no recorded value and no backfill is invented for them; any option that grandfathers will need an explicit owner assumption for the pre-242 history. | `letbuyy/harness1-supabase-faithful-bootstrap` | `7f6878b0` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-HARNESS-1 (this phase) | **The root cause behind three separate defects this session (CAP-C62, CAP-C64).** The executable suites bootstrap a database that differs from Supabase in three ways, each of which silently flips a test result: (1) `CREATE EXTENSION pgcrypto` with no `WITH SCHEMA` puts pgcrypto in `public`, so an unqualified `digest()`/`gen_random_bytes()` under `SET search_path TO 'public'` resolves when production raises — this hid the gift-card outage from baseline to migration 215 and kept CAP-C52/CAP-C53 open across three phases; (2) no `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES`, leaving **147 of 458** public tables reachable by no application role, which reads exactly like a live outage and nearly became one in F-TENANT-INV-1's report; (3) Postgres roles are **cluster-global**, so `CREATE ROLE … IF NOT EXISTS` inherits whatever an earlier suite left — this cluster's `service_role` had no BYPASSRLS, making "what can this role see" depend on which suite ran first. **What makes this tractable rather than 16 bespoke edits:** 13 of the 16 suites defining a bootstrap share a **byte-identical** block (sha `cb185874`), so the conversion is one exact block replacement repeated, verifiable by hash before and after. in_scope: a shared `tests/executable/supabase-faithful-bootstrap.mjs` carrying all three fixes with the reason for each; converting those 13 plus the two suites written this session; running **every** converted suite and reporting the result; and a governance ratchet with a registered legacy list that can only shrink. **Failures are findings, not rollbacks:** CAP-C64 states plainly that a suite passing today may be relying on the unfaithful state, so a suite that goes red after conversion is reporting something true about itself and will be recorded rather than reverted. Out of scope: converting `tenant-redteam-postgres.test.mjs`, whose bootstrap differs precisely because it already works around the cluster-global role hazard by dropping and recreating `app_tenant` — folding that into a shared module is a second problem, and the two remaining non-identical suites are registered, not rewritten. **Scope correction on completion:** the register came out at **25** legacy suites, not 2 — the first survey counted only files defining a `BOOTSTRAP_SQL` constant, and nine more create extensions inline — so this phase converts 14 of 39. | `letbuyy/rollback1-checkout-signature-precondition` | `e9418f63` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-06 | F-ROLLBACK-1 (this phase) | **CAP-C65, with the diagnosis corrected by measurement.** The backlog row said six checkout rollbacks carry a stale argument list. They do — but that is not the defect, because each restores the body **and signature of its own moment**, which was correct when written; migrations 233 and 234 later added `p_buyer_account_id` and `p_loyalty_points`, taking `create_checkout_order` from 18 arguments to 20, and no earlier rollback could be updated without destroying the historical body it exists to restore. **The defect is what Postgres does about it:** `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` with a different argument list does not replace, it **adds an overload**, silently and with no warning. The rollback reports success and every subsequent call fails `function public.create_checkout_order(...) is not unique`. A rollback is what gets run during an incident, so the recovery script converts a checkout problem into a total checkout outage. **Scope, swept rather than assumed** (every rollback file against the live signature of all 201 public functions, on a database with 000..245 applied): `create_checkout_order` is the **only** affected function — no other money RPC has the pattern — and **zero** functions currently carry a duplicate, so this is entirely latent. Six files, three shapes: 202 and 206 DROP the same stale arity they create; 213 DROPs an arity that has never existed; **215, 218 and 219 issue no DROP at all**. Nothing applies rollbacks automatically — human-run only. in_scope: a signature **precondition** in each affected rollback that refuses before touching anything, naming the live signature, the expected one, and the later migrations that must come off first; re-anchoring the two executable suites that apply historical rollbacks at head (the idempotency control needs "the live function minus the lock", so it should transform the current definition rather than replay 215's body; the purchase round-trip genuinely wants 219's rollback, so it should run it at migration 219 where it means something, **and** assert the refusal at head); a governance ratchet that every rollback creating a function either matches a live arity or carries the precondition; and executable proof both ways. Out of scope: rewriting any historical body — that would destroy what a rollback is for; and adding `tests/executable` to a gate lane, which is the wider gap this exposed and belongs to the owner, not to this phase — registered as CAP-C66. On the reference store that is **46 of 65 real orders (71%)**. So after this fix a merchant selecting all 65 visible "Pending" orders and clicking **Confirm** would update 19 and silently ignore 46. in_scope: expand `status=pending` to `.in(['pending','pending_cod'])` for the `orders` table only, leaving `status=pending_cod` exact for callers who ask for it; and make the bulk action report rows skipped as ineligible instead of omitting them. **Feedback lane applied explicitly** — `surface.sh` reports no lanes, but this changes a user-visible message, which the skill routes to the feedback lane; a tool's silence is not clearance. | `letbuyy/committed1-release-decrement` | `86aa4247` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-11 | F-COMMITTED-1 (this phase) | **CAP-C47 — `inventory_levels.committed_quantity` only ever grows.** `commit_inventory_reservation` (latest definition, migration 216) does `committed_quantity = COALESCE(committed_quantity, 0) + v_reservation.quantity`, and **no path anywhere decrements it** — verified across every migration, not just the two the backlog row named: `release_order_inventory` (216) has zero references; `expire_unpaid_committed_reservations` (211) has zero; and `release_order_inventory_with_ledger` (212) has exactly one, which is a column name in an inventory-events INSERT that *records* the value rather than changing it. A repo-wide grep for any decrement or `GREATEST` on the column returns nothing. So a released or expired commitment stays counted forever and the number drifts upward without bound. Its one consumer is `apps/merchant_app/lib/services/inventory_service.dart:26`, which shows it to the merchant as "committed". **No oversell risk** — `reserve_inventory` gates on `available`, which every release path does correctly restore; this is a reporting column that becomes progressively false. in_scope: migration 246 making the release paths decrement it as the exact inverse of the commit, plus executable proof on real Postgres that commit-then-release returns it to its starting value and that a second release is idempotent. **The precision that decides whether this fix is correct, found by reading rather than assuming:** `release_order_inventory` iterates **`order_items`**, not reservations, so it does not know whether a reservation was ever committed — a naive `- v_item.quantity` would decrement for an order cancelled while merely *reserved*, corrupting the column in the opposite direction. The decrement must be driven by the order's reservations with `status = 'committed'` and their own `quantity`, mirroring exactly what the increment used. Out of scope: any change to `available`, `reserved`, or the oversell gate; and backfilling the drift already accumulated in live rows, which is a data question needing its own measurement of how far off each row is. **7/7 executable on real Postgres, three consecutive runs** (one earlier run flaked on the last test in 55ms — an error, not an assertion — unreproduced and recorded as unexplained rather than cleared). **My own F-ROLLBACK-1 ratchet failed this branch** for a rollback restoring a function with neither a DROP nor a precondition; the precondition was added rather than the file exempted. | `letbuyy/qualify2-parameter-default-blind-spot` | `68801a7e` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-11 | F-QUALIFY-2 (this phase) | **CAP-C54 — the measurement that row demanded, plus the blind spot that made it unanswerable.** CAP-C54 asks a future phase to test `redrive_distributed_dead_letters`'s `p_recovery_operation_id text DEFAULT encode(gen_random_bytes(16), 'hex')` under the real application connection role before either fixing or clearing it, and explicitly says not to assume the outcome. Two findings already measured at `68801a7e` before claiming. (1) The row's premise does not match the repository: `database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:6937` declares that default as `encode(extensions.gen_random_bytes(16), 'hex'::text)` — QUALIFIED — and `git log -L` shows it has read that way since the baseline commit `67412652` (2026-06-02), before the row was filed. No later migration redefines the function. The row's hypothesis (that a parameter default is evaluated in the caller's search_path, which is why two superuser calls did not throw) may still be true, but it was invented to explain a non-event. (2) A REAL guard gap sits underneath it: `tests/executable/extension-schema-qualification-postgres.test.mjs:178` computes `bodyStart = definition.search(/\n(?:AS|BEGIN ATOMIC)\b/i)` and scans only `definition.slice(bodyStart)`, so the live database-wide sweep that proves every existing function is clean **cannot see a parameter default at all** — the header is sliced away before the scan. The forward ratchet (`tests/governance/extension-qualification-ratchet.test.mjs`) reads whole files and does cover the header shape, so the gap is in live truth, not in the forward guard. This phase: measures the actual PostgreSQL semantics of an unqualified pgcrypto call in a parameter default on real Postgres under both a Supabase-faithful database search_path and a restrictive role search_path; measures `redrive_distributed_dead_letters` from the live catalogue via `pg_get_functiondef` and calls it omitting the parameter as the real application roles; extends the live sweep to scan headers as well as bodies, with decoys in both directions; and closes or fixes CAP-C54 on whichever way the measurement goes. It does NOT widen any function's `SET search_path` — widening is not the fix (a SECURITY DEFINER search_path is a security control), and the transform-don't-retype rule from migration 244 applies if a fix proves necessary. Files: `tests/executable/extension-schema-qualification-postgres.test.mjs`, `CAPABILITY_BACKLOG.md`, this ledger, `EXECUTION_LEDGER.md`, and a migration + paired rollback ONLY if the measurement shows a live break. The runtime validator is `packages/checkout-core/src/index.ts`'s `StorefrontCreateOrderSchema` (:67), `StorefrontRazorpayOrderSchema` (:240) and `StorefrontRazorpayVerifySchema` (:247), each of which ALREADY exports its inferred type — `StorefrontCreateOrderInput` (:1816), `StorefrontRazorpayOrderInput` (:1817), `StorefrontRazorpayVerifyInput` (:1818). So the type checker validates these three handlers against a second, hand-maintained description of the wire contract rather than against the schema that actually parses the request. This is the third occurrence of the class CAP-C39 named and CAP-C42 hit: `StorefrontCreateOrderBody` claimed `shippingRateId?: string | null` while the real schema did not declare the field at all, so `z.object()` stripped it silently and a merchant's configured shipping rate was unreachable — TypeScript was green throughout because it was checking the wrong description. CAP-C46 declined to convert it for want of an audit of optional/nullable variance on the nested address fields; that audit is done and recorded here, measured at `7a76a2ce`: every sub-schema involved (`UuidSchema`, `EmailSchema`, `GstinSchema`, `IndianStateCodeSchema` in `packages/shared-types/src/index.ts:26,53,74,83`) resolves to `string`, `.default()` on `paymentMethod`/`deliveryOption` makes them REQUIRED in the inferred OUTPUT type (which is what `z.infer` gives and what the handler receives), and `.optional().nullable()` matches `?: T | null`. The three shapes are expected to be structurally identical today — the duplication is a FUTURE divergence hazard, not a present mismatch, and the typecheck is the oracle that proves it either way. This phase deletes the three local declarations, uses the exported inferred types, and adds a contract test that fails if any route file re-declares a local `type <Name>Body` while `@letbuyy/checkout-core` already exports `<Name>Input`. OUT OF SCOPE and filed, not fixed: the four hand-declared bodies in `services/api-gateway/src/routes/storefront/internal-payment-routes.ts` (:27,:35,:44,:55) and the four in `customer-engagement-routes.ts` (:32,:39,:45,:86) — those have no `<Name>Input` counterpart, so converting them means authoring schemas, which is a different change with runtime consequences. This phase enforces the two declared unions at the route and proves the guard runs BEFORE any database access, not merely that the status code is unfriendly. Measured at `5c084b5b`: **the registry is fine and the daemon's pull path is wedged.** Evidence, each a command: `https://public.ecr.aws/token/?scope=repository:supabase/supavisor:pull` returns **HTTP 200 in 0.86s** with a 1,593-byte token, and the manifest at `https://public.ecr.aws/v2/supabase/supavisor/manifests/2.9.7` returns **HTTP 200 in 0.89s** with that token — so DNS, TLS, auth and the image path all work from this machine. A direct `docker pull public.ecr.aws/supabase/supavisor:2.9.7` nevertheless wrote **zero bytes** of output and never produced the image. The discriminating test: `docker pull busybox:latest` — a tiny image never pulled here — behaved **identically**, empty log and no image. `docker system df` read `Images 18.46GB` at both ends of a sampling window in both cases. Meanwhile the daemon answers everything else instantly: `docker ps`, `docker images`, `docker version` (`29.6.2`), and `docker info` reports `ServerErrors` = `[]`. So the fault is inside the daemon's pull path, is NOT specific to supavisor, and is NOT the network. **Remedy: restart Docker Desktop — an owner action, not mine**, because it stops the four `edynox-*` containers belonging to a different project and the `letbuyy-executable-pg` container. **Bounded impact, stated so the urgency is not overread:** only work needing a NEW image is blocked. `pnpm test:executable` runs from the already-cached `postgres:16-alpine` and was observed passing 46/46 earlier in this same session, so the phase pipeline is unaffected. All stray pull processes were killed and their absence verified by `pgrep`; no container was created; the five running containers are untouched. NO CODE CHANGE: this phase rewrites CAP-C69 again and replaces the evidence document's E2E section again, stating plainly that two earlier diagnoses were published as settled and were not. There is no cap on the array. Measured at `1643e13a`: `database/supabase/migrations/000_baseline.sql:8914` declares `customer_wishlists.product_id` as **`text NOT NULL` with no foreign key to `products`** (whose own `id` at `:10584` is `uuid`), so the stored value is not constrained to a real product or even to a UUID shape; the only constraints are `customer_wishlists_customer_id_product_id_key UNIQUE (customer_id, product_id)` and FKs on `customer_id`/`store_id`. **Tenancy is NOT the defect and is not touched:** `store_id` comes from the body but `verifyStoreCustomer(c, body.store_id, body.customer_id)` gates every path, the table is registered with `tenantColumn: store_id`, and the `onConflict` matches the real unique constraint. The defect is write amplification and junk content: one authenticated request can create as many rows as it lists, each holding an arbitrary-length string. The repository's own precedent for a buyer-submitted array is `items: z.array(...).min(1).max(50)` in `packages/checkout-core/src/index.ts:57`, and its string fields carry `.max(...)`. The single-add route (`:1108`) has the same unbounded-string shape. **Enforcement is safe:** the only caller is `apps/customer_web/src/context/customer-auth-context.tsx:222`, which posts a browser `localStorage` wishlist through `apps/customer_web/src/app/api/customer/wishlist/sync/route.ts` — that proxy was read and correctly maps `productIds` to `product_ids`, so it is not another wire-contract divergence. **Deliberately NOT done, and why:** the ids are not required to be UUIDs. `products.id` is `uuid` so every legitimate id is 36 characters, and requiring the format would be the stronger fix — but the column is `text` on purpose, no live rows can be inspected from here (CAP-C69: the local stack cannot boot), and rejecting a shape existing clients may already send is a behaviour change this phase cannot prove safe. A length bound is strictly weaker and provably safe. Measured at `2420d803`, the validator names 7 tracked files, and every one was classified by pattern, line number and count without any value being displayed (Rule 5): six carry a Google `AIza` key — `apps/customer_app/{android/app/google-services.json, android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml, ios/Runner/GoogleService-Info.plist, lib/firebase_options.dart (×3), web/firebase-messaging-sw.js, web/index.html}` — all lineage-A Firebase client config that FB-2/3/4 retire; the seventh, `packages/dart/marketplace_services/lib/src/custom_payment_service.dart:89,122`, carries two **`rzp_test_`** keys (test mode, verified by reading only the mode prefix) in a donor fixture whose `name` field is `'Agrimate'`. **No live LetBuyy credential is among them.** | This phase writes NO code. It files what the measurement found, so the next reader inherits the attribution instead of re-deriving it. **Finding 1 — a structural blind spot in the scanner.** All five patterns in `scripts/validators/environment-validator.mjs:130-136` are prefix-anchored (`sk_/pk_/rk_`, `whsec_`, `rzp_`, `AIza`, PEM). A Razorpay **key_secret** has no prefix — it is 24 opaque alphanumeric characters — so the validator structurally cannot detect one. Verified that none exists today: both `RazorpayConfig` definitions obtain it via `String.fromEnvironment`, never a literal. The point is that the validator would not have told us either way. **Finding 2 — a client-side signing surface, measured not assumed.** `packages/dart/marketplace_core/.../razorpay_config.dart` exposes `keySecret` to client code (the `packages/dart/core` twin exposes only `keyId` — two definitions of one payment config, themselves a duplication), and two client services HMAC with it at `custom_razorpay_service.dart:154` and `advanced_razorpay_service.dart:206`. `String.fromEnvironment` is a **compile-time** `--dart-define`, not a runtime secret, so a value supplied that way is baked into the artefact. Reachability was measured rather than assumed: `advanced_razorpay_service.dart` IS exported from the package barrel at `letbuyy_marketplace_services.dart:59`, and `customer_app` imports that barrel (`main.dart`, `providers/auth_provider.dart`, `providers/user_provider.dart`) — but **no `customer_app` source references either service**, so nothing reaches the HMAC path today. Filed, not fixed: this is lineage A, which FB-2/3/4 move onto the canonical gateway, and changing payment-signature architecture is not a decision-free refactor. | **Finding 3 — a near-miss recorded so nobody repeats it.** Under naive redaction the lines `custom_razorpay_service.dart:18,66` and `advanced_razorpay_service.dart:80` read as `utf8.encode('<REDACTED>')` — the exact shape of a hardcoded HMAC key, in a payment file, in a package the shipped Play Store app depends on. It is not one. Each literal is 51 characters, non-alphanumeric, and carries **two `${…}` interpolations**: they are Dart templates for the HMAC *message*, not a key. Two intermediate measurements on the way there were ALSO wrong and were discarded rather than used — a sloppy regex that captured the identifier `RazorpayConfig.keySecret` and reported it as a 24-character literal, and an importer grep that failed on zsh globbing and printed `importers=0` when it had measured nothing. A botched measurement must not become a claim. Files: `CAPABILITY_BACKLOG.md`, this ledger, `EXECUTION_LEDGER.md`. | `letbuyy/gatediff1-persist-failure-sets` | `f9add428` | MERGED_DEVELOP | 2026-09-12 | F-GATEDIFF-1 (this phase) | **The rule every phase here runs on — *a gate COUNT is not a gate SET* — is enforced by hand, and the hand is unreliable.** Two measured problems at `f9add428`. (1) **`gate.sh` already knows the authoritative failing validator set and throws it away.** `:94` runs each validator and records failures by EXIT CODE into `$failed` — the ground truth — then `:97` prints it into the table through `cut -c1-110`. With today's two failures nothing is lost; with a longer list the gate's own output would silently drop names, which is the exact failure mode the rule exists to prevent, sitting inside the gate. Nothing persists the set to a file, so every phase re-derives it by grepping `$OUT/validators/*.log` for `failed|✖|Error:` while excluding `passed` — a heuristic that is **measurably wrong in the dangerous direction**. Compared against the exit-code truth across all 14 gate runs this session produced, it agrees on 13 and **under-reports on the fourteenth**: on the no-install run it named one failing validator when three had failed. The cause was read, not guessed — `cycle-budget-validator` and `route-ownership-validator` CRASHED with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, so their logs contain no pass marker and no failure marker, and a `has-failure-marker AND NOT passed` test therefore scores a **crashed validator as passing**. An exit code cannot make that mistake. (2) **A correction to this row's own first draft, recorded rather than edited away.** It claimed the `✖` extraction had missed INDENTED entries from nested subtests and that a later phase caught it. That was asserted, not measured, and it is **false**: a sweep of every `*.log` in all 14 run directories finds **zero** indented `✖` lines, so the whitespace-stripping in the new extraction has caught nothing and is defensive hardening for a shape `node:test` can emit, not a fix for an observed miss. The real second problem is smaller and still worth closing: the set was re-derived by hand in every phase, with the lane logs chosen from memory rather than swept. | `gate.sh` writes two files into every `$LETBUYY_GATE_OUT`: `failing-validators.txt` (from the exit codes at `:94`, untruncated, one name per line) and `failing-tests.txt` (every `✖` name across ALL lane logs, leading whitespace stripped so nested subtests are included, timings removed, the `✖ failing tests:` header excluded, sorted and deduplicated). A new `scripts/gate-diff.sh <baseOUT> <headOUT>` diffs both sets and exits non-zero on any delta. The table is left exactly as it is, truncation included — the table is a summary for a human, and the fix is to stop treating it as the evidence. | The control must show the new extraction catches what the old one missed, so it is run against a log this session already produced by accident rather than against a fixture: the no-install run of F-SECSCAN-1, whose test lanes died before emitting any `✖` at all, and the F-ROUTELEN-5 head run, which failed a nested subtest. For the order branches, measured: confirm reuses `payment-webhook:razorpay:<event>:paid`, and `post_ledger_transaction` (139) is `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on it; `money-rpc-idempotency-sweep` W1c executes five concurrent same-key confirms and gets one ledger transaction. Refund reuses its ledger key and has a paid-guard (`index.ts:422`). Failure posts no ledger. The outbox upserts on its key (`event-execution.adapter.ts:62-74`). (2) **Subscription activation is NOT replay-safe.** `subscription-execution.adapter.ts:49-127` cancels the active subscription, inserts a new one and records a new `billing_history` invoice, with no payment-id dedupe, and `billing_history.payment_id` is not unique. That yields two subscriptions, the verify one cancelled, and two invoices for one payment; the surviving row has no `external_subscription_id`. (4) **Point 3 of the brief:** `letbuyy/pay4-late-capture-after-release` (migration 252) is NOT merged. On develop a same-key replay is ledger-idempotent, but the cross-key double post LB-PAY-009 stays open until pay4 merges; pay4 should merge first. **Done (commits `89e4412a` fix, `bcf4a6c4` tests, `8cc5d5c2` lane move and pins):** a `failed` row is reclaimed by a conditional PATCH `processing_status=eq.failed`. A `received` row untouched for 5 minutes is reclaimed by `processing_status=eq.received&updated_at=lt.<cutoff>`, and the PATCH stamps `updated_at` itself. A duplicate arriving while an attempt is in flight answers 409; only `processed`/`ignored` rows are acknowledged as duplicates. The subscription branch skips activation when a subscription already carries the Razorpay payment id. **Evidence:** the route test (`tests/contracts/razorpay-webhook-retry-reclaim.test.ts`) mounts the real Hono app. 6 of its 10 tests fail on the unfixed route (before the fix, and again by `git revert --no-commit 89e4412a` after the move); 10/10 after. CONTROL 1: without the status predicate, all five do. CONTROL 2: with the trigger dropped and no explicit `updated_at`, all five stale reclaimers do; the statement's own stamp restores exclusivity. **The post-commit gate caught two defects of this phase, both fixed in `8cc5d5c2`.** First, the test first sat in `services/webhook-service/test` and imported tsx, which webhook-service does not declare (`dependency-manifest`, `undeclared-import`). Its new pins are stricter: the terminal-status set, the status-conditioned reclaim, the duplicate ack and the 409. The pin had been missed because a pre-commit `git grep` was capped at 20 lines and docs hits filled them. **CI finding (config reading; CI itself is unreadable from here):** webhook-service's test script passes `--experimental-strip-types`, which Node 20 does not have, and `ci.yml` pins `NODE_VERSION: "20"` and runs `pnpm turbo test` on changed packages. Migration 164 made `service_role` a member of `app_tenant`, whose default grants cover every table created after 164, so the 146 are the older tables that no migration granted explicitly: `users_custom`, `sessions`, `devices`, `verification_tokens`, `merchant_2fa_factors`, `auth_password_set_intents`, `payment_webhook_events`, `plans`, the employee tables, `collection_products`, and more. On any database built under the old default every grant is a no-op. It touches none of the 11 explicitly-granted tables, so the 165 and 236 money fences and the 4 deliberately narrow grants stay as they are, and a self-verification block asserts both. The rollback revokes the same list only where the database was built under the current default, and is a logged no-op otherwise. A new executable suite models the current default: it watches the 500's cause before 254, and after it proves zero unreadable relations, unchanged fences, idempotence and the rollback round trip. It also shows 254 is a no-op under the old default.
